===== 2026-07-01T22:27:08 claude-code 2848
please review this

## B23 Summary


Root cause: The lexer's  template_brace_balance  is global state that increments on every  {  and decrements on
every  }  it sees while in  IN_EXPR  mode — regardless of whether those braces belong to the template substitution
or to a nested construct (function body, object literal, destructuring pattern).

Why it fails for complex cases:

• For  ${a + b} : no nested braces, balance tracking works
• For  ${ {x:1} } : the object literal's  {  and  }  are counted, but the parser consumes the  }  as  RBRACE  via
expect(RBRACE) , so the template's  }  correctly returns  TEMPLATE_TAIL
• For  ${function(){return 1}()  or  ${({x: function(){...}}).x()} : the fun

The "new failing tests" are not actual regressions from the array.c3 fixes. 20 regressed tests all pass when run
individually. The root cause is heap pollution in the batch worker process — the  __resetGlobals()  /  hp.reset()

===== 2026-07-08T17:58:21 claude-code 2415
## Root cause found


The bug is in the compiler, not the runtime. Async functions are being compiled with  flags.is_async =
false  despite being declared with  async :


  FUNC_BUILD idx=1 is_gen=1 is_async=0 num_regs=5 is_arrow=0 has_rest=0
                         ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^  ^^^^^^^^^
                         async flag false  generator flag true


That's why the VM treats the function as a generator (no async): the slow CALL path allocates a
GENERATOR  HObject instead of a  Promise , the function body never executes, and the iteration variable
gets bound to that generator object instead of the return value of  makePending(i) . The  await  then
sees a non-thenable object, falls thro

# Update Config Skill

Modify Claude Code configuration by updating settings.json files.

## When Hooks Are Required (Not Memory)

If the user wants something to happen automatically in response to an EVENT, they need a **hook** configured in settings.json. Memory/preferences cannot trigger automated actions.

**These require hooks:**
- "Before compacting, ask me what to preserve" → PreCompact hook
- "After writing files, run prettier" → PostToolUse hook with Write|Edit matcher
- "When I run bash commands, log them" → PreToolUse hook with Bash matcher
- "Always run tests after code changes" → PostToolUse hook

**Hook events:** PreToolUse, PostToolUse, PreCompact, PostCompact, Stop, Notificat

## Task: Fix compound assignment `+=` string concatenation (23 test262 tests)

**Working directory**: /Users/rtomasi/cousas/duktape-c3/.worktrees/11-jul

### Root cause
In `src/compiler/expressions.c3` lines 230-268, when the compiler sees `x += 1`, it optimizes to `INC` or `INC_VAR` opcodes (numeric increment only). This bypasses the `ADD` opcode's string-concatenation semantics.

Per ES2015, `x += 1` is equivalent to `x = x + 1`. The `+` operator does string concatenation when either operand is a string. So `"1" + 1` should yield `"11"`, not `2`.

The INC optimization at lines 236-251 replaces the GETVAR with INC_VAR (line 243) or emits INC (line 245), which only does numeric increment.

## Task: Fix try/catch/finally completion values (10 test262 tests)

**Working directory**: /Users/rtomasi/cousas/duktape-c3/.worktrees/11-jul

### Root cause
The VM's completion value handling for try/finally is incorrect. Per ES2015 §13.15.8:

```
TryStatement : try Block Finally
1. Let B be the result of evaluating Block.
2. Let F be the result of evaluating Finally.
3. If F.[[type]] is normal, let F be B.
4. If F.[[type]] is return, or F.[[type]] is throw, return Completion(F).
5. If F.[[value]] is not empty, return Completion(F).
6. Return Completion{[[type]]: F.[[type]], [[value]]: undefined, [[target]]: F.[[target]]}.
```

The key rule: **if the finally block completes normally, the c
===== 2026-07-10T23:31:22.286000+00:00 opencode 1926
## Task: Fix Function constructor `.length` property (10 test262 tests)

**Working directory**: /Users/rtomasi/cousas/duktape-c3/.worktrees/11-jul

### Root cause
The Function constructor `new Function("arg1,arg2,arg3", null)` creates a function with `.length` = 1 instead of 3. The Function constructor should:
1. Concatenate all arguments except the last (which is the function body)
2. Parse the concatenated string as a comma-separated parameter list
3. Set `.length` to the number of formal parameters

Also: `new Function("arg1,arg2,arg3", "arg4,arg5", null).length` should be 5 (two arg strings concatenated: `"arg1,arg2,arg3,arg4,arg5"`).

Additionally, `.length` should be non-writable (read
===== 2026-07-10T23:31:29.364000+00:00 opencode 2669
## Task: Fix Boolean.prototype.valueOf/toString TypeError on non-Boolean `this` (10 test262 tests)

**Working directory**: /Users/rtomasi/cousas/duktape-c3/.worktrees/11-jul

### Root cause
In `src/builtins/boolean.c3`, both `builtin_boolean_proto_valueOf` (line 57) and `builtin_boolean_proto_toString` (line 40) silently return `undefined` when `this` is not a Boolean value or Boolean wrapper object. Per ES2015 §15.6.4.3 and §15.6.4.2, they should throw a TypeError.

Current code in `builtin_boolean_proto_valueOf` (line 57-71):

### Working directory
Work in `/Users/rtomasi/cousas/duktape-c3/.worktrees/wt1`

## Task: Fix Symbol well-known symbol property descriptors (Y1)

### Root Cause
In `src/builtins/symbol.c3`, the `register_symbol_constructor` function registers well-known symbols (Symbol.hasInstance, Symbol.iterator, Symbol.match, Symbol.matchAll, Symbol.replace, Symbol.search, Symbol.split, Symbol.toPrimitive, Symbol.toStringTag, Symbol.unscopables, Symbol.asyncIterator) with `PROP_FLAGS_NWC` which has `{writable:false, enumerable:false, configurable:true}`. Per ES6 §19.4.2, these should have `{writable:false, enumerable:false, configurable:false}` — i.e., `PROP_FLAGS_NNC`.

Not
===== 2026-07-19T22:47:11.499000+00:00 opencode 4797
You are working on the duktape-c3 JavaScript engine at /Users/rtomasi/cousas/duktape-c3/.worktrees/loop-19-jul

## Task: Make private class fields visible to direct eval (C7b)

### Root Cause
When `eval("this.#m")` is called inside a class method, the eval body compiles with a fresh `CompilerContext` that has NO private name context. The `compile_eval` function in `src/compiler/entry.c3` only receives `allow_super` — it does not propagate the caller's private-name context.

Per spec ES2022 §8.7.1, direct eval inherits the calling execution context's PrivateEnvironment.

### Architecture
- `src/compiler/entry.c3` — `compile_eval()` function creates a fresh `CompilerContext` for eval code
- `s
===== 2026-07-19T22:47:23.920000+00:00 opencode 4458

## Task: Fix try completion values + block-scope leave

### Bug 1: try completion values in eval mode

#### Root Cause
In `src/compiler/statements.c3`, the `try_statement` function handles try/catch/finally. When in eval mode, it tracks completion values via `eval_acc_reg`. The bug: when `break` fires inside a `finally` block with NO preceding expression, `eval_acc_reg` still holds the try block's last value instead of `undefined`.

# C3 Programming Language

C3 is a systems language — an evolution over C with modules, optionals, slices, defer, generics, and compile-time features. Compiler: `c3c` (LLVM backend). **Baseline: 0.8.0.** Naming conventions (PascalCase types, snake_case functions) are enforced by the lexer.

===== 2026-07-21T16:40:25+00:00 crush 1145
Investigate the X3 bug in the duktape-c3 JavaScript engine: writes to an enclosing function's local from a parameter-default expression are lost.

Repro: `function w(){ var p=null; (function(_ = p = 6){})(); return p; }` returns null instead of 6.

The issue is that the compiler's capture scan (which determines whether a local variable needs to be promoted from register to environment) doesn't walk into parameter-default expressions. So `p` stays register-resident in the outer function, and the inner function's default expression writes to the env copy instead.

Search for:
1. The capture scan code in the compiler (look for functions like `scan_captures`, `mark_captured`, `needs_env`, or sim

# C3 Programming Language

C3 is a systems language — an evolution over C with modules, optionals, slices, defer, generics, and compile-time features. Compiler: `c3c` (LLVM backend). **Baseline: 0.8.0.** Naming conventions (PascalCase types, snake_case functions) are enforced by the lexer.

===== 2026-07-29T20:48:23.541000+00:00 opencode 1071
Research the for-in enumeration bug in this C3 JavaScript engine. The bug: when using for-in, a non-enumerable own property should shadow an enumerable prototype property, but the engine enumerates both. Example:

```js
Object.create({p2:'p2'}, {p1:{value:'p1',enumerable:true}, p2:{value:'x',enumerable:false}})
// for-in should yield ["p1"] but yields ["p1","p2"]
```

Your group-E fix has a regression I found during validation. In your worktree /Users/rtomasi/cousas/duktape-c3/.worktrees/agent-1:

`eval('1; for (var a in {x:0}) { 5; }')` now returns `undefined`; spec (and main before your change) says **5**. 

Root cause: you emit `LDUNDEF eval_acc_reg` AFTER `loop_start = self.code_count`, i.e. AT the loop back-edge target, so every iteration re-executes the reset and wipes the body completion. Per spec (ForIn/OfBodyEvaluation), V starts undefined ONCE before iterating; each body evaluation then updates V (UpdateEmpty). The reset must be emitted BEFORE the loop-start label so it runs exactly once, before the first NEXTFOR.

# Data Visualization

A chart is **read by people and executed by you**. This skill turns "make it look
good" into a procedure with checks, so the result is right by construction rather
than by taste.

**The method here is design-system-agnostic.** Nothing in the procedure, the form
heuristic, the six checks, or the mark specs is specific to one product. A design
system supplies a small set of *parameters* (its ramps, a categorical order, a
diverging pair, a status palette, a texture, its surfaces, its filter components);
the method consumes them unchanged. A **validated de
===== 2026-07-31T17:18:20 claude-code 8834
Approach this as the design lead at a small studio known for their versatility, giving every client a visual identity pitched at the treatment the task actually calls for. Make deliberate choices about palette, typography, and layout that are specific to this subject, and avoid templated designs.

## Read the request first

Calibrate treatment, not whether to design. A doc deserves the same craft as a landing page — what changes is the treatment that craft is delivered in.
